# PART 13 — Organic Carbon Management — PART T: MICROBES & BIOFERTILIZERS (Final Session)

> Sources: 14 PPT slides (extracted from Word) + audio transcript (moderately corrupted; recovered from non-standard encoding). Gore opens by noting this is *"perhaps the last webinar"* of the organic-carbon series (**~24 sessions total**), and that he leaves for Africa until the 21st. **This session finally delivers three things promised earlier: the organic-culture preparation protocol (open since Part 13K), the VAM/mycorrhiza teaching (the gap flagged in Chapter 7), and the bacteria-into-jeevamrut timing.** ASR slips corrected inline: `पुटुरिया ऑरेंजिया` = ***Frateuria aurantia***; `व्याम` = **VAM**; `अरबोस्ट्राँग` = **Orgo Strong**; `एसके बायबीस/बायोडीज` = **SK Biofix**; `फिश्पा` = a Gujarat pharmaceutical company (name uncertain); `लाईफलायझेशन` = **lyophilization (freeze-drying)**; `बिवेरिया` = **Beauveria**; `मटेरायझम` = **Metarhizium**.

---

## 13T.1 What a microorganism is — and why results require multiplication

> **सूक्ष्मजीव हे आकाराने लहान जीव आहेत जे केवळ सूक्ष्मदर्शकाखाली पाहिले जाऊ शकतात.**
>
> *Microorganisms are small-sized organisms that can only be seen under a microscope.*

Gore makes a distinction that matters enormously for how you judge a biological input:

> ⚡ **THE FUNDAMENTAL DIFFERENCE FROM CHEMICALS —** *"If you spray a **fungicide** and a disease is present, it is controlled because the disease came into **direct contact** with the fungicide. If an insect is there and you use a **contact insecticide**, the moment it touches, it dies. **Microorganisms are not like that. They have to go there and they have to MULTIPLY.** Without multiplication we do not get results from them."*

This single point explains why biologicals so often "don't work": a chemical acts on contact, a microbe must establish a population first. **It also means a biological input applied into hostile conditions is simply wasted money** — a theme he returns to repeatedly.

### What a biofertilizer actually is

> ⚡ **DEFINITION —** *"A biofertilizer is **not a fertilizer**. There is no nitrogen in it, no phosphorus, no potash, none of the nutrients the crop needs. **But those nutrients — whether from the chemical fertilizer you applied, from organic matter, or already present in the soil particles — need these microorganisms to CONVERT them into available form.** If these microorganisms are not there, the fertilizer you applied just sits in the soil."*

The mechanism, stated precisely:

> ⚡ **INTERACTION RULE —** **microorganisms grow in the soil, and as they grow they produce various ORGANIC ACIDS. Those organic acids lower the soil pH and make the nutrients available.** *(This is the same weak-acid mechanism from Parts 13B and 13O, now stated as the defining function of a biofertilizer.)*

And on nitrogen fixers specifically:

> **"Azotobacter, Acetobacter, Rhizobium — these are nitrogen fixers. There is no nitrogen IN them, but they take nitrogen from the atmosphere and store it in the soil or in root nodules and make it available to the crop."**

---

## 13T.2 The two classes — biofertilizer vs biopesticide

The slide divides microorganisms into two uses:

| **जैविक खत — BIOFERTILIZER** | **जैविक कीटकनाशके — BIOPESTICIDE** |
|---|---|
| **यामध्ये जिवंत कार्यशील सूक्ष्मजीव असतात, जे वनस्पतींच्या वाढीसाठी आवश्यक असलेल्या विविध पोषक तत्वांच्या उपलब्धतेत मदत करतात** | **यामध्ये जिवंत कार्यक्षम सूक्ष्मजीव असतात, जे वनस्पतींवर येणाऱ्या विविध रोग आणि कीटकांना नियंत्रित करण्यास मदत करतात** |
| Contains living, functional microbes that help make the various nutrients needed for plant growth **available** | Contains living, effective microbes that help **control** the various diseases and pests that come on the plant |

> **His terminology note:** *"Rather than calling it कीटकनाशक (insecticide), it is better to say कीटनाशक (pest-killer) — because these do not only control insects. They control **nematodes** too, they control **fungi and bacteria** coming from your soil or onto your crop."*

### The four types of microorganism (सूक्ष्मजीवांचे प्रकार)

**बुरशी (Fungus) · जीवाणू (Bacteria) · विषाणू (Virus) · सूत्रकृमी (Nematode)**

> ⚡ **IMPORTANT DISTINCTION —** **"विषाणू (viruses) are NOT biofertilizers. Viruses are pesticides."** He names the examples: **HaNPV** (*Helicoverpa armigera* nucleopolyhedrovirus) for Helicoverpa, and **SlNPV** for *Spodoptera*. **Nematodes likewise appear on both sides** — harmful ones attack crops, while **entomopathogenic nematodes (EPN)** are beneficial and kill soil insects.

---

## 13T.3 Why use biologicals — the eight reasons

The slide lists why chemical over-reliance has forced this shift:

> **कृत्रिमरित्या तयार केलेली खते आणि रासायनिक कीटकनाशकांच्या अंदाधुंद वापरामुळे शाश्वत शेतीला धोका निर्माण झाला आहे:**
>
> *Indiscriminate use of artificially-made fertilizers and chemical pesticides has created a danger to sustainable farming:*

| # | Reason |
|---|---|
| 1 | **प्रदूषित पाण्याचे खोरे** — Polluted water basins |
| 2 | **मातीचे प्रदूषण** — Soil pollution |
| 3 | **सूक्ष्म जीव आणि मित्रकीटक नष्ट झाले** — Microorganisms and beneficial insects have been destroyed |
| 4 | **रासायनिक खते आणि रासायनिक कीटकनाशकांच्या वाढत्या किमती** — Rising prices of chemical fertilizers and pesticides |
| 5 | **रासायनिक कीटकनाशके पर्यावरणातील मित्रकीटकांना मारतात आणि कीटकांच्या पुनरुत्थानाचा धोका देखील असतो** — Chemical pesticides kill beneficial insects, and there is also the danger of **pest resurgence** |
| 6 | **पर्यावरणीय धोक्यांबद्दल वाढती चिंता** — Growing concern about environmental hazards |
| 7 | **जैविक खते आणि जैविक कीटकनाशकांचा दीर्घकालीन वापर किफायतशीर व पर्यावरणपूरक आहे तसेच अधिक कार्यक्षम आणि उत्पादक देखील आहे** — Long-term use of biologicals is economical and eco-friendly, as well as more efficient and productive |
| 8 | **सुरक्षित अन्न उत्पादन करण्यासाठी जैविक खते आणि जैविक कीटकनाशकांचा वापर करू शकतो** — Biologicals can be used to produce safe food |

**His health framing** (with the standing caveat that these are motivational claims, not epidemiology):

> *"We have completely wrecked our bodies. We cannot lift a hundred-kilo sack. Our daughters are not conceiving after marriage. **The WHO says India has a 32% sterility problem.** And if there is any solution, it is that we now have to eat pesticide-residue-free food."*

---

## 13T.4 The advantages — biofertilizers and biopesticides

| **जैविक खतांचे महत्त्व — Biofertilizer advantages** | **जैविक कीटकनाशकांचे महत्त्व — Biopesticide advantages** |
|---|---|
| **पर्यावरण सुरक्षित** — Environmentally safe | **कमी विषारी** — Low toxicity |
| **साठवणी दरम्यान 45°C पर्यंत जगतात** — **Survive up to 45 °C during storage** | **फक्त लक्षित कीटकांना (target pest) प्रभावित करते** — Affects only the target pest |
| **कमी खर्चात उपलब्ध होणारे** — Available at low cost | **अतिशय कमी प्रमाणात प्रभावी परिणाम** — Effective results at very low quantities |
| **बियाणे आणि मातीवर चांगले तग धरून राहतात** — Establish well on seed and soil | **सहज बायोडिग्रेडेबल, त्यामुळे कमी धोकेदायक आणि प्रदूषणासारख्या समस्या मोठ्या प्रमाणात टाळल्या जातात** — Easily biodegradable, so pollution problems are largely avoided |
| **वापरण्यास सोपे** — Easy to use | **एकात्मिक कीड व्यवस्थापनाचा (IPM) घटक म्हणून वापर केल्यावर प्रमुख कीटकांवर नियंत्रण ठेवताना पिकाचे उत्पादन जास्त राहते** — Used as an IPM component, yield stays higher while controlling major pests |

> ⚡ **THE 45 °C POINT MATTERS FOR YOU.** Gore addresses the obvious objection directly: *"Often we get this question — if it is that hot, will it work there? But when I speak on some technologies later, I will tell you what **spray drying** is, at what temperature it is heated, how it is put into a **dormant stage** — and there you can store it even at high temperature."*

---

## 13T.5 The classification of biofertilizers

| Function (जैविक खते) | Organisms |
|---|---|
| **नत्र स्थिर करणारे जीवाणू** — Nitrogen fixers | **रायझोबियम (Rhizobium), अझोस्पिरिलम (Azospirillum), अझोटोबॅक्टर (Azotobacter), अॅसिटोबॅक्टर (Acetobacter)** |
| **फॉस्फरस विद्राव्य जीवाणू** — Phosphorus solubilizers | **बॅसिलस (Bacillus), सूडोमोनास (Pseudomonas)** |
| **पोटॅश क्रिया वाढवणारे जीवाणू** — Potash mobilizers | **फ्रॅटुरिया ऑरेंटिया (*Frateuria aurantia*)** |

**Two additions from the transcript that are not on the slide:**

> ⚡ **ZINC SOLUBILIZERS —** *"There are also **zinc-solubilizing bacteria** — just as **Pseudomonas** releases your phosphorus, it also releases zinc."*

> ⚡ **CALCIUM MOBILIZERS — directly relevant to your soil.** *"I will name the company specifically. **SK Biofix** has bacteria that make **calcium available**. In soils where there is **calcium carbonate**, in **calcareous soils**, where the calcium level is quite good but it is fixed as calcium carbonate, or with phosphorus as **di-calcium phosphate and tri-calcium phosphate** — to make that available, these bacteria are now available in the market. If we use these bacteria, we can make the nutrients already present in our soil available **without adding any**. That reduces cost, reduces the chemicals we put on this planet, and keeps our soil healthy."*

### And the biopesticide classification

| Type | Organisms |
|---|---|
| **जैविक बुरशीनाशक** — Biofungicide | **ट्रायकोडर्मा विरिडी, सूडोमोनास फ्लोरोसेन्स, बॅसिलस सबटिलिस** |
| **जैविक कीटकनाशक** — Bioinsecticide | **मेटारिझियम अॅनिसोप्लिया, बिवेरिया बसियाना, व्हर्टिसिलियम लेकानी** |
| **जैविक सूत्रकृमीनाशक** — Bionematicide | **पॅसिलोमाइसेस लिलासिनस**, and **EPN** (entomopathogenic nematodes) |

> **Note the overlap he points out:** *"Now see — **Pseudomonas and Bacillus appeared over there in biofertilizers too.**"* The same organism can serve both functions.

> ⚡ **TRICHODERMA IS ALSO A BIOFERTILIZER —** *"Trichoderma is a **decomposing fungus**. It grows on the twig-waste you put in the soil, and whenever anything is decomposed, the nutrients in it become available to the crop. **That is why I call Trichoderma a biofertilizer too.** Any decomposing bacteria you use are also biofertilizers for your soil."*

---

## 13T.6 VAM / MYCORRHIZAE — the gap in the course, now filled

This is the most important new content in the session, and it addresses exactly what was flagged as missing back in Chapter 7.

> **What is VAM? वेसिक्युलर-आर्बस्क्युलर मायकोरिझा.** *In Greek: **Mykes = बुरशी (fungus)** and **rhiza = मुळी (root)**.*

### The two types

| Type | Where it grows |
|---|---|
| **एंडो मायकोरायझा — Endomycorrhiza** | **Grows INSIDE the root** |
| **एक्टो मायकोरायझा — Ectomycorrhiza** | **Grows ON the root and out into the soil** |

### How it works — the symbiosis

> **"This mycorrhiza grows on the root. The substances the root releases — the **carbon material** we already saw in previous sessions — it **feeds on that**. This is **symbiotic**: the things the crop does not want, the crop puts out, and it grows on those. And then it grows onward into the soil."**

### THE KEY FUNCTION — the moisture and nutrient extender

> ⚡ **THE CRITICAL MECHANISM —** *"**As far as there is air, and as far as there is moisture, VAM has the capacity to grow.** And if your crop's roots have reached only so far, and beyond that the moisture has reduced — **the VAM reaches to where the moisture IS, and brings that moisture back to the roots, and brings the nutrients from there back to the roots.** That is the size of the job this Vesicular Arbuscular Mycorrhiza does."*

**This is exactly right, and it is the single most valuable biological function for a water-limited, nutrient-locked soil:** mycorrhizal hyphae are far finer than roots and explore a much larger soil volume, effectively extending the root system many-fold.

### The visible test

> **"If you have done five-inch mulching in a fruit orchard and you lift it, you will see fine, fine, fine filaments on the roots. **That is all VAM**, growing in large quantity."**

### VAM suppresses soil-borne disease

> ⚡ **INTERACTION RULE —** *"Where VAM has grown in large quantity, the harmful fungi coming from your soil — **Fusarium, Ceratocystis, Rhizoctonia, Pythium, Sclerotium** — these various fungi **do not grow there. Because VAM grows in such large quantity, it COMPETES with them**, and because of that they do not grow."*

### VAM and nematodes — an honest, precise statement

> ⚡ **NEMATODE RULE —** *"And the second most important thing: **if you want to control nematodes, VAM must grow in your soil.** **VAM does not do anything directly to the nematode** — but where VAM grows well, where a really good healthy climatic condition stays in the soil, **there you see considerably fewer nematodes.**"*

**This is a careful and honest distinction** — VAM is not a nematicide; it changes soil conditions in a way that correlates with lower nematode pressure. Gore does not overclaim.

### His two favourite microorganisms

> ⚡ **HIS PICK —** *"I like two microorganisms very much. One is **Pseudomonas fluorescens**, which is called **PGPR — Plant Growth Promoting Rhizobacteria** — and one is **VAM**. And if these two microorganisms go into your soil in strength, and the environment there is favourable for their growth, **your land will not fail to stay fertile. It will not become infertile, because these keep working continuously in your soil.** There are others too, but these two are outstanding. **Use these always.**"*

---

## 13T.7 THE ORGANIC CULTURE PROTOCOL — the missing method from Part 13K

This is the answer to the open question from Part 13K, where the slide gave materials but no method. **Gore calls this Solid State Fermentation.**

### The materials (per acre dose)

| Material | Quantity |
|---|---|
| **Mixed oil cake** — neem + karanj + castor + mahua (sold as **Orgo Strong** or **Green Harvest**, from KRFG company) | **150 kg** |
| **Vermicompost (गांडूळ खत)** | **150 kg** |
| **Water** | 100–200 litres (for the culture mix) |
| **Jaggery (गूळ)** | **1 kg** |
| **All the microorganisms** (see below) | 1 litre each |

> ⚡ **THE CAKE RULE, RESTATED —** ***"Do not take neem cake alone.*** Take all of them mixed — neem, karanj, castor, mahua." *(Consistent with Part 13S: the four-cake blend, 100% de-oiled.)*

**The microorganisms to include:** *Trichoderma viride, Trichoderma harzianum, Pseudomonas, Bacillus, Verticillium, Metarhizium, Azotobacter, PSB, potash-mobilizing bacteria.* Buy them combined in one bottle if available, or as separate one-litre bottles.

### The site

> ⚡ **SITE RULE —** *"Do it on a **firm surface — not ploughed ground.** Soil is fine, but **firm** soil, because you have to mix it and water it and you don't want it mixing into the earth. **Under a tree's shade is fine if it is not the rainy season. If it IS the rainy season, you must 100% do this in a SHED. You cannot do it in the open.**"*

### The eight-step method

| Step | Instruction |
|---|---|
| **1** | Mix the **150 kg oil cake + 150 kg vermicompost** together and spread it out |
| **2** | **Water it — "enough that it forms a laddu"** (holds together when squeezed). **Take care that water does not drip out of it** |
| **3** | In **100–200 litres of water**, add **all the microorganisms** and **1 kg of jaggery** |
| **4** | **Leave that mixture only briefly**, then take it immediately and **sprinkle it over the wetted cake-and-vermicompost mix** |
| **5** | **Mix it thoroughly — "the way we mix cement"** — so every microorganism sticks to every particle and starts growing there |
| **6** | Form beds: **3 ft wide × 1 ft high, any length.** *"Do not make it wider than 3 ft; the length can be anything, but the height must not exceed 1 ft"* |
| **7** | **Cover with wet gunny sacking (ओला बारदाना)** to maintain moisture |
| **8** | **Do not go near it for 3 days** |

### The three-cycle turning schedule

| Day | Action |
|---|---|
| **Day 3** | Remove the gunny (put it to soak). **You will see different microorganisms grown, in different colours.** It will be **hot** — decomposition generates heat. If moisture has dropped, sprinkle a little water. **Mix like cement**, re-form the 3 ft × 1 ft beds, re-cover with wet gunny |
| **Day 6** | Repeat: uncover, mix, water if needed, re-form beds, re-cover |
| **Day 9** | **Final check** — see below |

### THE READINESS TEST — Day 9

> ⚡ **READY WHEN:** *"the **heat has dropped considerably**, it has turned **black in colour**, it is **light in weight**, and if there are clods and you break them, **lots of SPORES come out of them.**"*

> ⚡ **THE MAGGOT REASSURANCE —** *"And if there are flies around and they have laid eggs, you will see small larvae in it. **If you see those larvae, do not be afraid.** The fly lays eggs exactly where there are bacteria. **So do not be afraid — good bacteria have grown there, and your culture has turned out excellently.**"*

### APPLICATION RATES — by crop

| Crop | Rate |
|---|---|
| **डाळिंब — Pomegranate** | **1 kg per tree** |
| **द्राक्ष — Grape** | 0.5 kg per tree |
| **मोसंबी / संत्रा — Sweet lime / orange** | **2 kg per tree** |
| **केळी — Banana** | 0.5 kg per tree |
| **Any vegetable crop** | **300 kg per acre** (the full batch) |

### When it is essential

> ⚡ **INDICATION —** *"Wherever you have more soil problems — **sticky soils, soils where air does not circulate, soils where fungus has increased**, where you have grown **tuber crops like ginger or turmeric and wilt disease is coming — there is no alternative to organic culture.**"*

> **And his adoption advice, half-joking:** *"Do organic culture **just once**, and **don't give it away to ten people.** Those ten people will tell you a year later: what sin did we commit that we didn't get the organic culture?"*

### Direct application vs. multiplication — the honest comparison

> ⚡ **THE 80/100 RULE —** *"Many of you will be wondering: if we buy the kit directly and put it directly in the soil, won't that do? We put FYM, we put vermicompost, we put neem cake. **Absolutely it will do. No problem at all. But if you get 80% results from that, you will get 100% results from this. For that 20% there is a little effort — if you make it, you get the benefit.**"*
>
> **And the diagnostic bonus:** *"The second thing — **you will also find out whether there actually WERE microorganisms in what you bought.** If they were, they will grow, heat will be generated, and you will get satisfaction."*

### Ready-made kits he names

- **Vijaya Agro** (Sanam Mirchi ji) — kit available directly in the market
- **"Fasal Rakshak"** from **SK Biofix** — *"everything is given together in it"*
- Cake brands: **Orgo Strong** and **Green Harvest** — *"take good quality; do not take neem-lookalike material"*

---

## 13T.8 Adding bacteria to jeevamrut — the timing rule

This completes the slurry instructions from Part 13L.

> **सूक्ष्म जिवाणू स्लरी — जिवामृत देण्याच्या 1 दिवस आधी:**
>
> | Microbe | Quantity |
> |---|---|
> | **अझोटोबॅक्टर — Azotobacter** | **1 litre** |
> | **पीएसबी — PSB** | **1 litre** |
> | **केएमबी — KMB** | **1 litre** |
>
> **जीवामृताच्या टाकीमध्ये टाकावे, त्यानंतर दुसऱ्या दिवशी हे ड्रिपने किंवा बादलीने वापरून पिकाला द्यावे.**
>
> ⚡ **THE RULE —** ***One day before*** applying the jeevamrut, add these three to the jeevamrut tank; **the next day**, deliver it to the crop by drip or bucket.

**And the separation rule, restated:**

> ⚡ **"Here you CANNOT combine fungi and bacteria. Here only bacteria are to be added."** *(Consistent with Part 13L. He adds: if you want to apply fungi, they can be released directly.)*

**The precondition:** add the bacteria only **after the bubbling has stopped** — i.e. once the slurry has finished fermenting.

---

## 13T.9 Application methods (खते देण्याची पद्धत)

| Fertilizer form | Method |
|---|---|
| **दाणेदार खते / सेंद्रिय खते** — Granular / organic | **जमिनीवर टाकणे** — Broadcast on the soil |
| **विद्राव्य खते** — Water-soluble | **ठिबक** — Drip |
| | **फवारणी** — Spray |

**From the transcript:**

> ⚡ **FORMULATION-SPECIFIC RULES —**
> - **Direct broadcast:** you can mix the biofertilizer into granular chemical or organic fertilizer and broadcast it, then mix into the soil
> - **Liquid formulations** can go through the **drip**
> - ⚠️ **Talc-based powder formulations CLOG DRIPPERS** — do not use these through drip
> - ✅ **Dextrose-based formulations dissolve in water** — these can be used through drip
> - **Sprinkler:** *"Where you use a sprinkler, apply this without hesitation — it falls on the water and onto the soil, and they grow in your soil."*
> - **Foliar:** *"Some references say water-soluble biofertilizers are applied by spraying too — Dr. Krishnachandra's books mention it, and there are international articles. **But I will not recommend it yet.** For now we should give it through the soil."*

---

## 13T.10 THE FORMULATION TECHNOLOGIES — how biologicals are actually made

This is unusually technical for a farmer-facing session and it is genuinely useful for judging product quality.

### The evolution of carriers

| Generation | Carrier | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| 1st | **Charcoal powder** (5 kg packets per acre) | *"Results did not come. That is why microorganisms got a bad name."* |
| 2nd | **Talc powder** (government-distributed) | *"People used them and there was simply no confidence in them."* |
| 3rd | **Liquid formulations** (separate bottles per organism) | Better — but depends entirely on how spores are stabilised |
| 4th | **Dextrose-based powders** | Water-soluble, drip-compatible |
| 5th | **Lyophilization (freeze-drying)** | The most advanced |

### How spray-dried products are made

> **"How is this made? The bacteria are grown, they multiply in **liquid**; in the **spray dryer** the liquid is burned off, and at that temperature the **spores are harvested** — which are **alive but in a DORMANT STAGE.** Those dormant-stage spores are then put into **dextrose** at whatever count is needed."**

> ⚡ **THE DOSE-CONCENTRATION RULE — genuinely useful for judging a product:**
> - **A 0.5 g/litre dose means CONCENTRATED** — high spore count, low dextrose
> - **A 2 g/litre dose means fewer spores, more dextrose**
> - *"Because in that droplet of water, that many spores must fall on the insect."*
>
> **His recommendation:** *"Where you have to give it by **spraying**, I will always say **use the concentrated one.** Where you have to give it **through the soil**, more dextrose is fine — **because that dextrose helps their multiplication there.**"*

### The colour test — a free quality check

> ⚡ **QUALITY TEST —** *"Your microorganism's colour comes through to the dextrose's colour. Dextrose is white. **Suppose you want Trichoderma viride — Trichoderma viride is a GREEN-coloured fungus. Your powder must be green.** Only then can you say Trichoderma viride is in it. Small things.**
>
> **If you got a plain white powder and someone tells you Trichoderma viride is in it, I will say — perhaps it has Beauveria or Verticillium in it, whose colour is white. Trichoderma is not in it.** Because the spore's colour is the colour that comes through."*

### Lyophilization — the newest technology

> **"**Lyophilization** is a technology where the spores are made hard in dextrose — made so hard that the moment you put them in water they dissolve immediately. There is a large Gujarat pharmaceutical company doing this. They make microorganisms by lyophilization technology, which stay so hard in any soil and any temperature that **until they come into contact with water they do not activate.** You will find what looks like a **stone** in it. And when you put that stone in water it dissolves, and all the microorganisms inside come out."**

### Activating a liquid formulation before use

> ⚡ **ACTIVATION RULE —** *"When a **liquid** comes, I always tell you: put a little **dextrose** in it, or **Glucon-D**, or good **organic jaggery** — **keep it half an hour to an hour, and then use it. Activate it before using and you will get very good results.** For dextrose-based powder formulations, we do not need to add this."*

---

## 13T.11 EFFICIENT USE AND PRECAUTIONS — the closing slide

> **जैविक खते, जैविक कीटकनाशकांचा कार्यक्षम वापर आणि खबरदारी:**

| # | Precaution |
|---|---|
| 1 | **साठवण** — **Storage: store in a cool, dry place away from sunlight and heat** |
| 2 | **योग्य स्ट्रेन किंवा योग्य जैविक खत वापरावे — Use the correct STRAIN / correct biofertilizer.** Examples: **अ. शेंगावर्गीय पिकांसाठी — रायझोबियम** (legumes → Rhizobium); **ब. ऑसिटोबॅक्टर — ऊस** (Acetobacter → sugarcane); **क. अझोटोबॅक्टर — डाळिंब** (**Azotobacter → POMEGRANATE**) |
| 3 | **जैविक खत आणि जैविक कीटकनाशकांमध्ये इतर रसायने मिसळताना काळजी घ्या** — Take care when mixing other chemicals with biologicals |
| 4 | **कालबाह्यता तारखेपूर्वी वापरा, ताजे तयार असलेले वापरा** — **Use before the expiry date; use freshly prepared material** |
| 5 | **चांगले परिणाम मिळविण्यासाठी जैविक खते आणि जैविक कीटकनाशके वापरताना प्रक्रिया आणि वापरण्याच्या पद्धतीचा अवलंब करा** — Follow the proper process and method of use |
| 6 | **जैविक खते आणि जैविक कीटकनाशके ही पूरक उत्पादने आहेत आणि पर्यायी नाहीत — म्हणून रासायनिक खते, कीटकनाशके आणि सेंद्रिय उत्पादनांसोबत त्यांचा वापर संतुलित ठेवा** — **Biologicals are SUPPLEMENTARY products, NOT substitutes — so keep their use balanced alongside chemical fertilizers, pesticides and organic products** |

> ⚡ **PRECAUTION 2 IS DIRECTLY FOR YOU: Azotobacter is the named biofertilizer for POMEGRANATE.** *(Rhizobium is for legumes with nodules — cowpea, soybean, chickpea, tur, groundnut. Azotobacter is for non-legumes and orchard crops.)*

> ⚡ **PRECAUTION 6 IS THE COURSE'S HONEST POSITION —** *"**NEVER substitute biofertilizers for chemical fertilizers.** Do not directly stop chemical fertilizer and say I will run only on microbial fertilizers. **No. You must slowly INCREASE the biologicals and DECREASE the chemicals.** But the first condition is: **organic matter must be used in large quantity.**"*

---

## 13T.12 THE CLOSING ARGUMENT — the three conditions

Gore ends with what is effectively the summary of the entire organic-carbon block, stated as a chain of preconditions.

### The core warning

> ⚡ **"If you are NOT putting organic matter into your soil — no neem cake, no karanj cake, no Orgo Strong or Green Harvest, no FYM, no Gayatri Sanjeevani compost — then, with folded hands, my request: DON'T TOUCH microorganisms. Let the shopkeepers and the companies say what they like. If you are putting living microbes into soil that has no organic matter, YOU ARE WASTING YOUR MONEY."**

### The proportionality rule

> ⚡ **THE CHAIN —** **"The more organic matter, the more microorganisms you use. The more microorganisms, the less chemical you will have to use** — because microbial growth increases the efficiency of the chemical fertilizer you applied. So you can reduce the chemical dose — **but only if there is organic carbon in the soil.**"

### The forest endpoint

> **"And a time will come when, after putting organic matter into your soil, you will not need to add living microbes again. **Your land will become like a forest — where nobody goes and applies PSB, but there is never a phosphorus deficiency, without any DAP.** That is the land you have to create."**

### The critical limitation — nothing from nothing

> ⚡ **THE HARD RULE —** *"**If there is no phosphorus in your soil, however much PSB you release, it will make no difference. If there is no potash in your soil, however much potash-mobilizing bacteria you release, the crop will not get potash.** Why do I say this? Because the PSB you gave — Bacillus or Pseudomonas — multiplied there and released organic acids, and those organic acids **make soluble the phosphorus that was FIXED and not soluble. But if there is no phosphorus there at all, what will it make soluble? It cannot.**"*
>
> **"So I say: **if the nutrients are in the soil, these will work. And if organic matter is in the soil, these will work.**"*

### Copper — the final repetition

> ⚡ **"COPPER KILLS THEM IMMEDIATELY. That is why I keep saying: NO copper in the soil. Because when you put copper in the soil, you will kill the microorganisms in your soil."**
>
> **The acceptable exception:** *"Give copper at such a low concentration that it works as a **mineral nutrient**. In Avena Nutrimix, only 2.5 kg goes into a 52 kg bag, and that lasts two acres for a full year. It goes as a nutrient. **But if you are putting it there as a FUNGICIDE, in that same area, that is not right.**"*

### His marigold story — the schedule trap

> **"I grew marigold after 25 years. A marigold-growing farmer came to me and asked: how many drenching applications have you done? I said I have not applied any fungicide. He said — why? He said not one of your plants has died. I said what is the connection between fungicide and a plant dying? He said the fungi in the soil kill the crop.**
>
> **He said: by now you should have had FOUR drenchings, four times fungicide into the soil. I said I do not put fungicide in the soil — it makes the land infertile. He said marigold simply cannot be grown without it, the plant dies. I said fine, when it starts dying I will apply it.**
>
> **THIS IS 'THE SCHEDULE'. No, no — you MUST apply it four times. Why? And after applying it, even if my marigold survives, IT WILL KILL MY SOIL. That thought I never entertain."**

### The trial instruction

> ⚡ **HIS STANDING TEST —** *"From the first day until now I have said only one thing: **whatever I tell you, run a trial on it. Leave ten trees. Shut off one line's valve. Until harvesting, do not use the formula I am telling you on that one line. And if you are getting results, then follow B. T. Gore. Otherwise not. Otherwise not.**"*
>
> **"If I blindly trust someone, if I become a blind devotee and act the moment I am told, and do nothing sensible myself — then I have no faith in my own knowledge. It is more in the other person."**

---

# Analysis — Part 13T

## The session that finally fills three gaps

**1. The organic-culture protocol (open since Part 13K).** The full method is now here: firm shaded site, 150 kg cake + 150 kg vermicompost, wet to "laddu" consistency, microbes + 1 kg jaggery in 100–200 L water sprinkled over, mixed like cement, 3 ft × 1 ft beds under wet gunny, turned on days 3, 6 and 9. **And the readiness test is specific and checkable** — heat dropped, black, light, spores when you break a clod. The maggot reassurance is a nice touch: fly larvae are evidence the culture worked, not that it failed.

**2. VAM / mycorrhizae (the gap flagged in Chapter 7).** He now covers it properly, and his description of the key mechanism is **correct and important**: mycorrhizal hyphae extend beyond the root zone to where moisture and nutrients still are, and ferry them back. On a water-limited, nutrient-locked calcareous soil, this is the single most valuable biological function available. **His nematode statement is admirably precise** — VAM does nothing directly to nematodes, but healthy VAM-rich soil correlates with lower nematode pressure. He does not overclaim.

**3. The bacteria-into-jeevamrut timing** — one day before application, bacteria only, never mixed with fungi.

## The genuinely valuable technical content

**The dose-concentration rule is the best product-evaluation tool in the course.** A 0.5 g/L dose means high spore count; a 2 g/L dose means more dextrose filler. Use concentrated for foliar, dextrose-rich for soil (where the dextrose feeds multiplication). This is real formulation literacy.

**The colour test is a free, immediate quality check.** *Trichoderma viride* is green — if your "Trichoderma" powder is white, it is not Trichoderma. Simple, and it will catch adulterated product.

**"Microbes must multiply, chemicals act on contact"** is the framing that explains every biological failure — and it justifies both the multiplication protocols and the insistence on organic matter first.

**The hard limitation is honestly stated:** PSB cannot solubilise phosphorus that is not there. Biologicals unlock what exists; they do not create nutrients. This is the correct and commercially inconvenient truth.

**Calcium-mobilizing bacteria for calcareous soil** is a genuinely relevant lead for your block — bacteria that release calcium fixed as calcium carbonate and as di-/tri-calcium phosphate. That is precisely your phosphorus-lockup mechanism.

## Cautions

| Item | Assessment |
|---|---|
| **"WHO says India has 32% sterility"** | Not a WHO figure in that form. Infertility prevalence estimates for India vary widely (roughly 10–15% of couples). Treat as rhetorical. |
| **The 45 °C storage claim** | Reasonable for **dormant spore** formulations (which is his point about spray drying and lyophilization). **Not true for live liquid cultures**, which degrade in heat. Read it as formulation-specific, not universal. |
| **Product and company names** | Vijaya Agro, SK Biofix ("Fasal Rakshak"), Orgo Strong, Green Harvest, KRFG, the Gujarat lyophilization company. He names them openly; apply the same scepticism as before. His own Avena Nutrimix appears again in the copper discussion. |
| **Foliar biofertilizer** | He explicitly **declines to recommend it** despite citing references — that is the appropriately conservative call. |
| **Talc clogs drippers** | Correct and practically important. |
| **"Forest needs no PSB"** | True as an endpoint image, but forests also have very different nutrient cycling and no harvest export. An orchard removes nutrients in fruit every year; it will never be fully self-sufficient. |

## For Pulivendula

1. **Azotobacter is the named biofertilizer for pomegranate** (precaution 2 on the final slide). Rhizobium is for your legume cover crops and green manure; **Azotobacter for the trees.**

2. **Prioritise VAM at planting — this is now doubly confirmed.** It was my recommendation in Chapter 7 and Gore now names it as one of his two favourite organisms. On your soil its two functions matter most: **extending the root system to reach moisture and locked nutrients**, and **competing out Fusarium, Ceratocystis, Rhizoctonia, Pythium and Sclerotium.** Inoculate at planting when the root system is forming.

3. **Chase the calcium-mobilizing bacteria lead.** SK Biofix reportedly has bacteria that release calcium fixed as CaCO₃ and as di-/tri-calcium phosphate. **That is exactly your phosphorus-lockup mechanism** (Chapter 8). Worth investigating specifically — but demand evidence, and trial it on a portion.

4. **Make organic culture at least once, and do it properly.** At 1 kg per pomegranate tree, a single 300 kg batch covers 300 trees. For 2,000 trees you would need about seven batches — substantial, so start with a trial block. The 80%-vs-100% honesty is fair: direct application works, multiplication works better.

5. **Use the two quality tests on every biological you buy.** The **colour test** (green powder for Trichoderma viride) and the **dose-concentration rule** (0.5 g/L = concentrated, 2 g/L = dextrose-heavy). Buy concentrated for spray, dextrose-rich for soil.

6. **Never put talc-based formulations through your drip.** They will clog emitters — and per Chapter 12, emitter clogging is already a risk on your water.

7. **Take the hard rule seriously:** biologicals unlock what is present. Your three-layer soil test (Part 13J/Chapter 10) tells you what is actually there. **If phosphorus is genuinely absent rather than locked, PSB will not help** — you would need to add phosphorus, in rock-phosphate form co-composted (Part 13J).

8. **Adopt his trial protocol.** Leave one line's valve shut; run ten trees without the formula through to harvest; compare. That is the correct standard, and it applies to his recommendations as much as anyone's.

---

## Addendum to the running Interaction-Rules master list (§13C.5)

Add these from Part 13T:

> ⚡ **MICROBES MUST MULTIPLY; CHEMICALS ACT ON CONTACT.** A biological applied into hostile conditions is wasted money — this is the fundamental difference.
> ⚡ **Biofertilizers contain NO nutrients** — they produce **organic acids** that lower pH and convert existing soil nutrients into available form.
> ⚡ **HARD LIMIT: PSB cannot solubilise phosphorus that is not there.** Biologicals unlock what exists; they do not create nutrients.
> ⚡ **VAM extends beyond the root zone to where moisture and nutrients remain, and ferries them back to the root** — the key function on water-limited soil. It grows as far as there is air and moisture.
> ⚡ **VAM outcompetes Fusarium, Ceratocystis, Rhizoctonia, Pythium and Sclerotium.** It does **nothing directly** to nematodes, but VAM-rich soil shows markedly fewer nematodes.
> ⚡ **Azotobacter → pomegranate; Rhizobium → nodulating legumes; Acetobacter → sugarcane.** Match the strain to the crop.
> ⚡ **Bacteria into jeevamrut ONE DAY before application** (Azotobacter + PSB + KMB, 1 L each). **Bacteria only — never fungi and bacteria together.**
> ⚡ **DOSE = CONCENTRATION:** 0.5 g/L dose = high spore count (use for spraying); 2 g/L dose = more dextrose (fine for soil, where the dextrose aids multiplication).
> ⚡ **COLOUR TEST:** the product's colour is the spore's colour. *Trichoderma viride* is GREEN — a white powder sold as Trichoderma is probably Beauveria or Verticillium.
> ⚡ **Talc-based formulations CLOG DRIPPERS; dextrose-based dissolve** — only the latter through drip.
> ⚡ **Activate liquid formulations before use:** add dextrose/Glucon-D/organic jaggery, wait 30–60 min, then apply.
> ⚡ **Organic culture readiness (day 9):** heat dropped, black colour, light weight, spores released when clods are broken. **Fly larvae present = good sign** (flies lay where bacteria are).
> ⚡ **Organic culture rates:** pomegranate **1 kg/tree**; grape 0.5; sweet lime/orange 2; banana 0.5; vegetables 300 kg/acre.
> ⚡ **BIOLOGICALS ARE SUPPLEMENTARY, NOT SUBSTITUTES.** Increase biologicals and decrease chemicals gradually — never switch abruptly.
> ⚡ **NO ORGANIC MATTER → DO NOT BUY MICROBES.** Applying live microbes to soil without organic matter is wasted money.

---

*Translated through Part 13 (Parts A–T). **THE ORGANIC-CARBON BLOCK IS NOW COMPLETE** — approximately 24 sessions covering: fertility vs productivity, the carbon cycle and allocation model, the causes of carbon loss, the ten methods of building carbon, and now the microbes that drive it all. **Gore's stated roadmap from here: soil testing → fertilizer types → correct usage and compatibility → fertigation → foliar application.** Still outstanding: the dedicated pH session, the nutrient-by-nutrient deep dives, water conditioners, growth regulators, and the monthly Maharashtra vegetable calendar.*

# APPENDIX — Upcoming Session Preview: NITROGEN (from the introductory recording)

> **Source note:** this preview is reconstructed from the tutor's own words in the very first (introductory) recording, where — while explaining how the year would be structured — he laid out exactly what the dedicated **nitrogen session** would cover. It was captured only in the raw audio and is recorded here so that when the nitrogen session actually arrives, you know the sub-points it is expected to close. Nothing here is new agronomy; it is a table of contents for a session not yet delivered.

In the opening session, describing what a full weekly session on a single nutrient would look like, the tutor said the **nitrogen session** (the first of the nutrient-by-nutrient deep dives) would answer, in order:

1. **Where does nitrogen come from?** — its sources, and the ~78% present in the atmosphere.
2. **What is nitrification?** — the microbial conversion process, and the nitrifying bacteria (*Nitrosomonas* → *Nitrobacter*) that drive it. *(Cross-reference: introduced in Part 7 — urea → ammoniacal N → nitrite → nitrate — and expanded there.)*
3. **How does it dissolve in water, and how does it reach the roots?** — the pathway from atmosphere/soil into the transpiration stream. *(Cross-reference: the mass-flow mechanism, Part 4.)*
4. **How does it convert between forms, and how is it finally made available to the crop?** — ammoniacal → nitrite → nitrate → uptake, and why the conversion stalls when nitrifying bacteria are absent. *(Cross-reference: "why urea stopped working," Part 7.)*
5. **If nitrogen becomes excessive, what do we do?** — the management of over-supply, and which nutrients an excess suppresses (potassium, copper, boron) and how it degrades produce quality and invites disease. *(Cross-reference: the excess-nitrogen antagonism rule, Parts 9 and 12.)*
6. **If nitrogen is deficient, how do we recognise the deficiency symptoms** — and which other nutrients become unavailable as a consequence.
7. **Volatilisation and leaching** — how to keep nitrogen in the soil rather than losing it to the air; and the warning that nitrate leaching into groundwater and drinking water is a genuine cancer risk. *(Cross-reference: the nitrate-in-water caution, Parts 2 and 8.)*

> ⚡ **INTERACTION RULES this session is expected to formalise** (already stated in scattered form across Parts 4–12, to be consolidated here when it arrives):
> — **Excess nitrogen → suppresses potassium, copper and boron → soft, disease-prone, lodging growth, poor fruit set.**
> — **Nitrogen conversion requires nitrifying bacteria; no bacteria → the form-conversion stalls and applied urea does nothing.**
> — **Nitrate that leaches below the root zone is a triple loss: wasted fertiliser, polluted groundwater, and a drinking-water cancer risk.**

**For Pulivendula:** when this session arrives it will matter most for the bahar programme — nitrogen timing drives the vegetative-versus-reproductive balance (Part 13B's carbon-allocation model), and on your calcareous, high-EC soil the volatilisation/leaching management and the nitrate-in-borewell-water caution are the two points to read closely.
